# Architecture Decision Records

Short, immutable records of decisions that are expensive to reverse. One file per
decision, numbered `ADR-NNNN-<slug>.md`. Never edit an accepted ADR — supersede it
with a new one and mark the old `Superseded by ADR-NNNN`.

## ADR number allocation

ADR numbers are drawn from **per-lane reserved ranges**; a lane never allocates
against the shared high-water mark. This removes the last-writer-wins race that
made this file a serialization point for the whole board (in one afternoon an
OP-101 card was renumbered three times and the OP-96 lane four times, each cycle
costing a rebase, a CI run and often a re-review). The rule, its rationale and
the rejected alternatives are recorded in
[ADR-0300](ADR-0300-adr-number-range-allocation-policy.md).

- The orchestrator is the **allocator**. It assigns each lane (`OP-<n>`) a
  disjoint range of numbers up front — before any of that lane's cards are
  dispatched — and writes the range into every card body of the lane.
- A lane takes its next ADR number as the **next unused number inside its own
  range** (a lane-local counter). It never consults `origin/main`'s high-water
  mark, the tail of this file, or another lane's refs.
- A new lane's block starts **above the highest in-flight range end** —
  `max(range ends in flight) + 1`, never `max(consumed number) + 1`: a lane's
  `Next free` cell is a consumed count, not its reservation bound
  ([ADR-0300 §10](ADR-0300-adr-number-range-allocation-policy.md)).
- A reserved range is **exclusive to its holder**, gaps included. A dependent
  ticket (one whose story builds on the holder's code) continues its parent
  lane's range; an independent ticket gets its own block.
- Ranges are disjoint by construction and are never re-cut, so two lanes cannot
  collide even though neither inspects the other's branches.
- Gaps inside a range (and ranges that go unused) are expected and cheap: a
  number is consumed once and is never reissued to another lane.
- Numbers allocated **before this policy** (`≤ 0145`) are frozen history and are
  never moved; a branch that already carries one keeps it.
- Board-wide / process decisions that belong to no single lane draw from the
  allocator-owned `board-wide` range.

_Frozen pre-policy numbers held by in-flight refs:_ `0143` (OP-96 F2, PR #221),
`0139`–`0141` (OP-96 round-3), `0131`/`0132` (OP-97 RED, PR #215), `0111`/`0112`
(OP-94 mobile skip-channel). These stay as committed; their lanes draw any **new**
ADRs from their reserved range above. The OP-97 GREEN implementation
(`t_58825ec3`) was first drafted as `0135` under the retired rule and renumbered
to `0180` (the first number in this lane's range) once ADR-0300 landed on `main`.

_Board-wide / process numbers consumed so far:_ `0300` (ADR-0300, the allocation
policy), `0301` (ADR-0301, the split `audit` gate — card `t_1bf1ea8d`, PR
[#230](https://github.com/yahodu/openpic-webapp/pull/230)), `0302` (ADR-0302,
the guard's contract — `t_2ae626e9`), and `0303` (ADR-0303, the
workflow-shell-safety guard + the `pr_checks.yml` `env:` transport and the OP-101
F1 GREEN review sign-off — card `t_734fb1a0`, PR #240 merge `c44c311`), and `0304`
(ADR-0304, the stale-draft-PR reconciliation rule — card `t_e43b1ff0`, branch
`OP-101-task-stale-draft-pr-reconciliation`), and `0305` (ADR-0305, the CI-evidence
rule — a PR conflicting with `main` starts no `pull_request` run, so evidence must
be a run on the head SHA — card `t_f141ecd8`, branch
`OP-101-task-ci-evidence-guard-adr`), and `0306` (ADR-0306, the stale-branch
deletion rule — a branch whose content is a subset of `main` is deleted — card
`t_c362aaf8`, branch `OP-101-task-stale-branch-deletion`), and `0307` (ADR-0307,
the unmerged-PR rule — a card must not complete on an unmerged deliverable, and an
open PR is declared in the handoff and gets a follow-up merge lane — card
`t_892df2ea`, branch `OP-106-task-pr299-docs-tighten`), and `0308` (ADR-0308, the
one-owner-per-branch landing rule — one card owns a branch's landing and its
fallback/verification card is a `parents=[primary]` child, not an independent
sibling; card `t_58139e6e`, branch `OP-110-task-one-owner-per-branch-adr`), and
`0309` (ADR-0309, the `git config` read-vs-write identity guard — read identity
only with `--get`, write it only in an explicit local scope, never `--global`,
and set the repo-local identity explicitly when provisioning a checkout/worktree;
card `t_cdacca2f`, branch `OP-110-task-git-identity-guard-adr`), and `0310` (ADR-0310, the git identity
guard's pinned contract — card `t_54545281`), and `0311` (ADR-0311, the
`pin-hooks-path` warning pin — card `t_60e20c44`), and `0312` (ADR-0312, the
same-lane ADR-card serialization rule — card `t_851ad543`). Next free in the
block is `0313`.

_The **board-wide** [`0308`](ADR-0308-one-owner-per-branch-landing.md) record gained a **§Recurrence** section (card `t_1deaf426`, orchestrator; branch `OP-111-task-adr-0308-recurrence`) after the OP-111 GREEN landing (`t_71cc5e0c`, PR #352) reproduced the one-owner hazard in a new, **run-level** shape — a live implementer worker mutated the lane worktree after its handoff while the review run owned the same path. No number is consumed: the block cell stays `0311`._

_The **board-wide / process** [ADR-0312](ADR-0312-same-lane-adr-card-serialization.md) record takes `0312`, the range slot the allocator reserved at card creation (card `t_851ad543`, decided by the orchestrator card `t_78773f59`, branch `OP-112-task-same-lane-adr-serialization` off `origin/main` `a2de1a2`), and the block cell advances `0312` → `0313`. It records the rule the intra-lane number collision kept outrunning after [ADR-0300](ADR-0300-adr-number-range-allocation-policy.md) removed the cross-lane one: §7, §8 and §9 are **read-side** rules, so two cards of one lane claimed in the same dispatcher tick enumerate the same snapshot and mint the same integer — which is what happened at `0584`, where `t_48a8e8ce` (PR [#377](https://github.com/yahodu/openpic-webapp/pull/377), landed first) and `t_c469c799` (renumbered to `0585` by comment) both minted `ADR-0584` off the trunk cut `c055cac`, the fourth recurrence of a class already seen at `0147`, `0382`/`0383`+`0384` and `0482`. The decision is **rule C**: same-lane ADR-minting cards are serialized by the task graph — the successor is a `parents=[…]` child of its predecessor and reads its number off the merged trunk, so there is no concurrent sibling to enumerate — with the allocator, worker and reviewer contracts and the two rejected alternatives (a reserve-first trunk ledger edit; a `reserved:` marker in this file checked by the guard) recorded in the ADR. No number outside `0300`–`0319` is touched._

| [0620](ADR-0620-op118-access-links-qr-red.md) | OP-118 RED: access-link rotation and QR generation pinned — the share-URL/QR-payload builders and the size/format validator, `POST /events/{e}/access-links:rotate` (confirmation 422, organizer-only 403, one active link with the old `revokedAt`, the §4.2 `{accessLinks, warning}` body, `event.link.rotated`), and `GET …/{slug}/qr` (one content-addressed `mediaAssets` export, signed `/o/` URL, 410 for a revoked slug) | Accepted |
| [0621](ADR-0621-op118-access-links-qr-green.md) | OP-118 GREEN: access-link rotation and QR generation implemented — the rotation as one transactional server-side array update (`findOneAndUpdate` pipeline + `returnDocument: "before"`, `event.link.rotated` in the same transaction, `422 acknowledgement_required` decided in the handler), the content-hash QR cache (one `exports` object, one `mediaAssets` row, the signed `export` URL persisted on the row so a repeat answer is byte-identical), the `410 access_link_revoked` contracts/transport append, `pdf` admitted by `buildObjectKey`, and the `qrcode`/`pdf-lib` dependencies | Accepted |
| [0622](ADR-0622-op118-qr-cache-pins.md) | OP-118 review round 1 pins: the QR cache hides the work (the render no longer runs on a repeat `(slug, format, size)` request) and is scoped to `kind: "export"` (a byte-identical `thumbnail` row is neither reused nor stamped with `qrUrl`/`qrExpiresAt`) | Accepted |
| [0623](ADR-0623-op118-qr-cache-green.md) | OP-118 GREEN (review round 1): the QR cache read runs before the render on a new render-free `mediaAssets.qrCacheKey` `(eventId, slug, format, size)` (a hit skips the `qrcode`/`pdf-lib` work and re-signs nothing), both the read and the upsert are scoped to `kind: "export"`, and the upsert absorbs the racing `E11000` (retry once, then swallow when a foreign-kind row holds the unique slot); a supporting `(tenantId, kind, qrCacheKey)` index is added | Accepted |
| [0624](ADR-0624-op118-rotate-refusal-pin.md) | OP-118 review round 1 TEST: a refused access-link rotation (`409 conflict`) writes nothing — the stored `accessLinks` keep their single revoked element and no `event.link.rotated` reaches the outbox (F1) | Accepted |
| [0625](ADR-0625-op118-rotate-refusal-green.md) | OP-118 GREEN (review round 1): the rotation refuses before it writes — the `findOneAndUpdate` filter carries `accessLinks: { $elemMatch: { active: true } }`, a `null` result is the `409 conflict` raised inside the transaction before any document update or `event.link.rotated`, and the normal path (transaction, pipeline, `returnDocument: "before"`, §4.2 body) is byte-identical | Accepted |
| [0640](ADR-0640-op119-members-analytics-red.md) | OP-119 RED: the event member list and tenant analytics pinned — the inclusive 366-day range validator (`range_too_large`/`invalid_date_range`/`validation_failed`) and the totals reducer (sums + `max` p95), `GET …/analytics` (flat ascending series from `analyticsDaily` only, reduced totals, owner\|admin, the 400-day 422) and `GET …/events/{e}/members` (masked contacts of the event's workers with their cached upload counts, co-organizer admission, non-oracle 404) | Accepted |

| [0642](ADR-0642-op119-members-analytics-green.md) | OP-119 GREEN: the analytics read is one indexed `analyticsDaily` `find` (closed `scope` vocabulary, a malformed query `eventId` = `422`, `scope=event` bounded to the path tenant, totals from the pure reducer) and the member list is the event's workers with `maskEmail`-masked contacts and the cached `uploadedImageCount`, admitted through `resolveEventActorRole`; `COLLECTIONS.analyticsDaily`, `range_too_large` + its transport row and the two §20.1 indexes added | Accepted |
| [0643](ADR-0643-op119-members-alias-pins.md) | OP-119 review round 1 pins: the member list's `current` tenant alias (200 + `X-Tenant-Id`), the malformed member rows that 500'd (no join instant, no `userId`), and the malformed query `eventId` = 422 characterization | Accepted |
| [0644](ADR-0644-op119-review-round1-green.md) | OP-119 review round 1 GREEN: the member list runs the shared `tenantStage` with a new event-scoped `admission` seam (`admitEventActor`), so `current` resolves and the denial is logged; the member projection is total (a row that cannot name a user is skipped, one with no join instant falls back to `_id.getTimestamp()`); `AnalyticsQuery` is a discriminated union that removes the unreachable tenant fallback | Accepted |
| [0660](ADR-0660-op120-invitations-red.md) | OP-120 RED: the co-organizer invitation surface pinned — invitee normalization (lowercase email / E.164 phone / hex user id), the sha256 token hash and its lookup filter, the `coorganizers.per_event` seat count over active members plus pending invites, `POST`/`GET …/events/{e}/invitations` (201 nine-key body, duplicate 409, seat 403, self 422, existing member 409, required idempotency), the idempotent `DELETE` revoke and the public `GET /invitations/{token}` preview (404 for an unknown token) — with the raw token proven absent from the response, the outbox and every value, present only in the rendered invitee message | Accepted |
| [0661](ADR-0661-op120-invitations-green.md) | OP-120 GREEN: the co-organizer invitation surface implemented — the async projection/access modules and the synchronous `sendTransactionalNow` delivery that puts the one-time link in the invitee's own message (ADR-0660 A9 corrected: the outbox fan-out cannot address an account-less email/phone invitee), `tokenHash`-only storage with the raw token minted at dispatch time and never persisted, the event-membership `GET` list (a co-organizer needs no `tenantMembers` row), the idempotent `DELETE` revoke, the public five-key preview, the three `COLLABORATION_ERROR_CODES` appends and the §13.6 `tokenHash` unique plus duplicate-live-invite partial indexes | Accepted |
| [0662](ADR-0662-op120-gap-pins.md) | OP-120 round-1 gap pins: log redaction, phone invitee, event-scoped list, concurrent duplicate 409, revoked preview | Accepted |

| [0664](ADR-0664-op121-invitation-decisions-green.md) | OP-121 GREEN: the invitation decision surface implemented — `decideTerminalOutcome` (pure mapper: replay `200` with the original channel, `410` for revoked/expired, fail-closed expiry, throws on an unknown status) and `matchInvitee` (case-insensitive user id, trimmed email, E.164 digits, `contactVerified` per contact flag); `POST /me/invitations/{id}/accept|reject` and the public `POST /invitations/{token}/accept|reject` (`?via=sms`, `401` with a token-specific `loginUrl` + masked preview and the token unconsumed, `403 invitation_not_for_you`, `403 account_incomplete`, `410 invitation_revoked|expired`, `404`); the accept transaction (conditional `findOneAndUpdate` + `event_organizers` upsert `co_organizer` + `collab.invite.accepted`, one row and one emit under a concurrent pair); the shared `closeInvitationAffordance` that marks `actions[].state = unavailable` / `actionTarget.state = resolved` on decisions **and** on the OP-120 revoke; `DELETE …/events/{e}/members/{userId}` (`204`, `status: removed` **and** `revokedAt`, originating invitation revoked, `409 cannot_remove_organizer`); the event-detail read admitted through `admitEventActor`; and the four `COLLABORATION_ERROR_CODES` appends | Accepted |
| [0665](ADR-0665-op121-invitation-decisions-red.md) | OP-121 RED: the invitation **decision** surface pinned — the terminal-state mapper (`decideTerminalOutcome`: accepted/rejected replay `200` with the original `resolvedVia`, revoked/expired `410`, a live pending row re-read, never `409`) and invitee identity matching (`matchInvitee`: case-insensitive user id, case-insensitive trimmed email, E.164 phone with separators, and the `contactVerified` flag I9 refuses on); `POST /me/invitations/{id}/accept|reject` and the public `POST /invitations/{token}/accept|reject` (`?via=sms`, `401` with `loginUrl` + `inviteePreview` and the token unconsumed, `403 invitation_not_for_you`, `410 invitation_revoked|expired`, `404` for an unknown token, `403 account_incomplete` for an unverified matching contact), the accept transaction that inserts exactly one `co_organizer` row (never a second organizer, even from a row that names `organizer`), the §2.3 rule-3 affordance closure measured on the real feed (`actions[].state = unavailable`, `actionTarget.state = resolved`, `actionResolvedVia`), the concurrent pair that must produce one membership row and one `collab.invite.accepted`, and `DELETE …/events/{e}/members/{userId}` (`204`, `status: removed` **and** `revokedAt`, the originating invitation revoked, `409 cannot_remove_organizer`) with the event read as the co-organizer's positive control and its loss after removal as the negative one | Accepted |
| [0680](ADR-0680-op122-queue-port-red.md) | OP-122 RED: the provider-neutral queue pinned — the strict §10.4 pointer envelope (ten keys, extra field refused), `queueKeyFor`/`defaultPriorityFor` (`selfie` → `q:selfie` at `high`), the Upstash Redis-REST adapter (one `LPUSH`, Zod-parsed answer, a changed shape = `UpstreamContractError` + `queue.upstream_contract_violation`, a 5xx = retryable `TransportError`), the `memory` adapter's claim/ack/nack semantics, and the non-fatal `enqueueWork` that records `processing.queueRef` only | Accepted |
| [0681](ADR-0681-op122-queue-green.md) | OP-122 GREEN: the queue port implemented — the strict §10.4 envelope with the two total mappings written as closed-union conditionals (no `Record` index, so no `detect-object-injection`), the Upstash **list** adapter (`LPUSH` head / `RPOP` tail with the typed count, adapter-minted `randomUUID` ids, a process-local claim ledger so `ack`/`nack` can act on a popped payload, `nack` requeued immediately with the delay marked as a shortcut, drift logged as `queue.upstream_contract_violation`), the memory adapter's single entry table with `visibleAtMs`/`inFlight`/`done`, and `enqueueWork`'s single `try` over the enqueue and its `processing.queueRef` write returning `{enqueued: false}` + `warn queue.enqueue_failed` and never throwing | Accepted |
| [0682](ADR-0682-op122-queue-followup-pins.md) | OP-122 round-1 review follow-up (test-only): P1 the pointer validated **before** the broker (`enqueueWork` may not enqueue what `queueEnvelopeSchema` rejects), P2 the `timeoutMs` request budget (abort → retryable `TransportError` with `code: "timeout"` and no drift record), P3 the process-local ack/nack ledger, P4 the retry-ordering divergence (memory in place, Upstash last — the port guarantees delay-bounded availability only), P5 `nack`'s ignored `delaySeconds`, P6 the one-poison-payload batch loss (recovery blocked on OP-136) | Accepted |
| [0683](ADR-0683-op122-queue-followup-green.md) | OP-122 round-1 review follow-up GREEN: `enqueueWork` builds the `ObjectId` and parses `queueEnvelopeSchema` **before** `queue.enqueue` (a §10.4-rejected pointer can no longer reach the broker; the failure takes the same `warn queue.enqueue_failed` / `{enqueued:false}` path as a broker failure), the Upstash adapter gains an optional `timeoutMs` (default the documented `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000`, `AbortController` + `clearTimeout` in a `finally`, abort → retryable `TransportError` `code: "timeout"` and no drift record), the foreign-id `ack`/`nack` stays a silent no-op over a process-local (unbounded, documented) ledger, and the port states its retry contract (FIFO over enqueued + delay-bounded availability, position adapter-defined; the stricter "back" rule is not taken because the P4 pins freeze both directions and this card may not edit a pin) | Accepted |
| [0684](ADR-0684-op122-queue-retry-position-and-containment.md) | OP-122 round-1 sign-off residue (test-only): **F4** the retry position is unified on the "back" rule — a nacked message becomes claimable behind the messages enqueued after it, so the memory P4 pin is retargeted to `[second, first]` and `memoryWorkQueue.nack` must move the entry to the back (the Upstash list already does it by `LPUSH`ing to the head while `RPOP` drains the tail); **F6** one poison payload no longer costs the claimed batch — `claim` parses the whole `RPOP` batch, re-`LPUSH`es every §10.4-valid payload in popped order before raising one `error` `queue.upstream_contract_violation` (`operation: "claim"`), and drops only the poison (re-pushing it would loop the key forever); **F5** the Upstash `nack`'s ignored `delaySeconds` is frozen at 0/600/3600 s, the delayed sorted set named as the upgrade that would honour it | Accepted |
| [0685](ADR-0685-op122-queue-retry-position-and-containment-green.md) | OP-122 round-1 sign-off residue GREEN: **F4** `memoryWorkQueue.nack` splices the entry out of its `entries` table and pushes it to the back, so a retry becomes claimable behind everything already enqueued on its key (still gated by `visibleAtMs = now + delaySeconds`), matching the Upstash list — the port's `nack` doc and the `memory-queue.ts` header lose the old "adapter-defined / redelivers in place" divergence (ADR-0683 §4's sentence is superseded, the frozen record untouched); **F6** `createUpstashQueue.claim` parses every payload of the one `RPOP` batch before acting, re-`LPUSH`es every §10.4-valid payload **in popped order** (which restores FIFO, since `LPUSH` prepends and `RPOP` drains the tail) before it raises exactly one `error` `queue.upstream_contract_violation` (`provider: "upstash"`, `operation: "claim"`, the `queueKey`), drops only the poison (re-pushing it would drift forever), keeps the clean path and the per-payload `claimed` ledger exact, and still raises on an all-poison batch | Accepted |
| [0686](ADR-0686-op122-queue-claimed-ledger-and-mid-rescue-pins.md) | OP-122 round-1 follow-up (test-only): **P7** the `claimed` ledger maps **each** `messageId` of a claimed batch to **its own** payload, so `nack`ing the second of two messages re-`LPUSH`es the second's bytes instead of a duplicate of the first (pinned on the wire and on the next `claim`, with the two ids asserted distinct); **P8** a broker failure **mid-rescue** (`RPOP` ok, `LPUSH#1` ok, `LPUSH#2` 503) rejects `claim` with a retryable `TransportError` (`code: "http"`, `status: 503`) and **not** an `UpstreamContractError`, records **zero** `queue.upstream_contract_violation`, reports no partial success, and leaves the already-rescued payload claimable (asserted with `toContainEqual`, so F7 may still recover the dropped one); **P9** a batch holding two invalid payloads of **different** classes raises exactly one drift whose message names the **first** class (`A queued message was not JSON.`), with both valid payloads back in FIFO order and a third `claim` empty. Characterization pins: green on arrival, each proven load-bearing by a one-token mutation of `upstash-queue.ts` (`1 failed | 20 passed` apiece, adapter restored byte-identically) | Accepted |
| [0687](ADR-0687-op122-queue-nack-doc-delay-exception.md) | OP-122 round-1 follow-up GREEN (comment-only): the `nack` docblock in `apps/web/src/server/queue/work-queue.ts` now guarantees the **retry position** — a nacked message becomes claimable behind everything already enqueued on its key (ADR-0685 §2), the same position in every adapter, since `memory` splices the entry to the back and the Upstash list `LPUSH`es back to the head while `RPOP` drains the tail — and states the **delay** honestly: honoured by `memory`, still ignored by the Upstash list, which re-pushes at once and serves the payload immediately at any delay until the delayed sorted set (`ZADD` + `ZRANGEBYSCORE`) lands (F5, frozen by ADR-0684 §3). ADR-0685 §3's sentence is refined, its §2 decision stands; no behaviour, wire, test or adapter change | Accepted |
| [0700](ADR-0700-op123-upload-config-batch-resolve-red.md) | OP-123 RED: the upload pre-check pinned — `@/server/uploads/batch-resolve` (`resolutionFor` folding one hash into `completed` / `in_progress` / `not_found` / `rejected` with a fixed precedence where a stored asset wins, `quotaSufficiency` as the advisory `remaining >= requested` rule, `isValidBatchId` for a UUIDv4 or 24-hex id, and the claim guard that reuses the upload guards' own extension/MIME table so `.NEF`+`image/tiff` passes), `GET /api/v1/uploads/config` (the contract's eight parameters, three of them read from `platformSettings.upload`, `private, max-age=300`) and `POST …/events/{eventId}/uploads/batch-resolve` (tenant-wide `completed` dedupe with `linked`, per-user `in_progress` resume whose parts come only from `ListParts`, `not_found` with the threshold-derived mode, per-file `rejected`, the advisory `quota` block, `413 payload_too_large` at 501 hashes, the new `409 upload_window_closed` with `details.canExtend`, and a read-only pre-check that writes no session and emits no per-file notification) | Accepted |
| [0701](ADR-0701-op123-upload-config-batch-resolve-green.md) | OP-123 GREEN: the upload pre-check implemented — the pure module exactly as pinned, `GET /uploads/config` reading the three tunables from `platformSettings.upload` with `private, max-age=300`, and `batch-resolve` on `rateLimitIdentity` + `tenantStage({admission: eventActorAdmission(3)})`: tenant-wide asset dedupe vs per-user session resume, parts only from a live `ListParts` (a failure is `502 bad_gateway`), the advisory `quota` from `checkEntitlement(…, 0)`, `413` at 501, the new `409 upload_window_closed` appended through `MEDIA_ERROR_CODES`/`MEDIA_ERROR_TRANSPORT`, and one `info` line with per-state counts, never a file name | Accepted |
| [0720](ADR-0720-op124-single-put-complete-link-only-red.md) | OP-124 RED: the single-PUT upload, the completion transaction and the link-only attachment pinned | Accepted |
| [0721](ADR-0721-op124-single-put-complete-link-only-green.md) | OP-124 GREEN: the single-PUT upload, its completion transaction and the link-only attach implemented — the two pure rules, the three routes, the shared upload-window rule, the 30-minute in-flight grace, `409 size_mismatch`, the storage port's `getObjectRange`/`deleteObject`, the memory adapter's opaque-token presign plus its test-support `PUT` route, the memoized `getWorkQueue()`, and a minimal `GET …/images` | Accepted |
| [0740](ADR-0740-op125-multipart-uploads-red.md) | OP-125 RED: the multipart upload endpoints for Uppy's `@uppy/aws-s3` hooks pinned | Accepted |
| [0741](ADR-0741-op125-multipart-uploads-green.md) | OP-125 GREEN: the six Uppy `@uppy/aws-s3` hooks implemented — the two pure rules plus `PART_SIZE_BYTES` in `@/server/uploads/multipart`, the session-bound handle resolver that maps every refusal to `404`, the five event-scoped routes (`create`/`listParts`/`sign`/`complete`/`abort`, with the offsets the behaviour actually demands — ADR-0740's table is off by one on four rows), one `loadMultipartUpload` preamble for the four calls after `create`, `409 parts_missing` derived from the **declared** part count before the provider is asked to assemble, `409 upload_expired` normalized from the provider's `NoSuchUpload`, the idempotent `204` abort, `presignPart`/`abortMultipart` on the `ObjectStorage` port and the R2 adapter (an absent `Parts` element is `[]`, not drift), the OP-124 registration transaction extracted to `@/server/uploads/complete-upload` and shared, and both new codes appended to `MEDIA_ERROR_CODES`/`MEDIA_ERROR_TRANSPORT` | Accepted |
| [0760](ADR-0760-op126-batch-status-notifications-red.md) | OP-126 RED: the batch status read and the upload lifecycle notifications pinned — `deriveUploadBatchPhase` and the user-safe `describeUploadFailure` table, `GET …/uploads/batches/{batchId}` with the phase-derived `files`/`pipeline`/`failures`/`stalledSessions` blocks, and `checkUploadBatchTransition` emitting one summary per (batch, uploader) plus the `upload.blocked.quota` pair to the uploader and organizer | Accepted |
| [0761](ADR-0761-op126-batch-status-notifications-green.md) | OP-126 GREEN: the batch read and the upload lifecycle emissions implemented — one snapshot composes §5.5's eight keys and feeds `deriveUploadBatchPhase`, `files.total` counts the batch's `upload_sessions`, `failedUpload` counts an aborted/failed/expired session (a pending one keeps the batch `uploading`, which is what I1's live read demands) while `duplicatesSkipped` is derived from the completed sessions that wrote no join row — without it a deduped completion leaves the batch `uploading` forever, `batchId` queries match both the `ObjectId` and the string shape, `checkUploadBatchTransition` emits one summary per (batch, uploader) and the quota-block refusal is caught in the shared completion so both completion paths emit it once per event per six hours, and the two indexes are added with `event_images` leading on `tenantId` so the index lint keeps its test-owned exception list | Accepted |
| [0762](ADR-0762-op127-event-images-management-red.md) | OP-127 RED: the event image curation surface pinned — `buildEventImageListFilter` (the §4.4 filters + §0.8 compound cursor), `resetProcessingForReprocess`, the full `GET …/images` projection (signed 24 h thumbnails, `imageMeta` dimensions, nullable thumbnail for a pending upload, counter summary), `PATCH`/`DELETE …/images/{id}` (hide is not delete; one delete transaction for `refCount`, both counters, the `storage.bytes` release and the matched attendees' `matchCount`), `POST …/images/{id}/reprocess` (`409 not_failed` only), `POST …/images:reprocess-failed`, and the 401/404/403 ladder | Accepted |
| [0763](ADR-0763-op127-event-images-management-green.md) | OP-127 GREEN: the curation surface implemented — the list projection is its own DTO in `@/server/images/dto` (`toEventImageListItem`, 15 keys, no `assetId`) so OP-125's completion pin is untouched, the thumbnail is a per-item §5.6 `/t/` signature with a 24 h life and is **nullable** until the derivative exists (contract `interface EventImage` updated to `string | null`), the `summary` takes `done`/`failed` from the event counters and `queued`/`processing` from a live tally with `total` as their structural sum, `uploadedBy` is 24-hex validated in the filter builder before the `ObjectId` cast (F7) and a foreign cursor stays `400 invalid_cursor`; `PATCH` writes only `visibility`; `DELETE` is one transaction (soft delete, `face_matches` rows removed with each profile's `matchCount` decremented, `refCount - 1` without a purge, `$inc` on the **nested** `counters.imageCount`/`counters.storageBytes` — a top-level `$inc` is a silent no-op — and the `storage.bytes` release); `POST …/reprocess` refuses a non-`failed` image as `409 not_failed` (appended to `MEDIA_ERROR_CODES`/`MEDIA_ERROR_TRANSPORT`) before any enqueue and answers `202` with the reset item, the bulk route answers `{ requeuedCount }` for this event's failed images only; `POST …/images/originals:archive` is left to OP-133 (F4) | Accepted |
| [0764](ADR-0764-op127-event-images-followup-pins.md) | OP-127 follow-up TEST: the RED review's three gaps pinned — `?uploadedBy` validated as 24-hex before the `ObjectId` cast (`422 validation_failed`, not a `500`) while a well-formed value still narrows the page, the shared-asset delete (`refCount 2 → 1 → 0` with the row and its `storage`/`derivatives` untouched and the other event still served), and the route-level filter wiring I7 could not see (`?visibility` and `?status` each narrow an unbounded three-image page, and the two are `$and`-composed) | Accepted |
| [0765](ADR-0765-op127-delete-idempotency-pin.md) | OP-127 follow-up TEST: the repeat `DELETE` of one image pinned as idempotent for a shared asset — the first call leaves `refCount 1` (with the join row soft-deleted, event A's nested counters at `0` and the tenant's `storage.bytes` meter at one image's bytes), and the second call on the **same** image id is `404 not_found` with the asset `refCount` still `1`, the row and its `storage`/`derivatives` intact, the first call's `deletedAt` stamp not rewritten, the nested counters and the meter unchanged, and the other event still listing its image (a status-only `404` is not enough: a probe that answers `404` after committing the decrements is caught by the state arms) | Accepted |
| [0766](ADR-0766-op128-batch-signed-media-url-refresh-red.md) | OP-128 RED: the batch signed-media-URL refresh pinned — `POST /api/v1/media/signed-urls` (max 200 items, `413 payload_too_large` from the handler because the pipeline's own validation status is `422`), the per-item decision as a pure seam (`authorizeSignedUrlItem`: `not_found` → `authentication_required` → `feature_not_available` → `forbidden`, with the §5.6/ADR-0762 `visibility` predicate denying a **hidden** image to a matched attendee while the visible match in the same request still signs), the aggregate `signedUrlBatchStatus` (`403` only when every item is denied **and** none is `authentication_required`, which is what lets I6's anonymous original stay `200` with a `denied[]` entry), the `public` label that per-item authorization needs, and a query-count pin via `Collection.prototype` spies (`reads(200) === reads(2)`) | Accepted |
| [0767](ADR-0767-op128-batch-signed-media-url-refresh-green.md) | OP-128 GREEN: the batch refresh implemented — `@/server/media/signed-urls` (`authorizeSignedUrlItem` with the pinned precedence, `signedUrlBatchStatus` with the `authentication_required` exemption; `code` stays optional because the U1 shape pin reads it on the type) and `POST /api/v1/media/signed-urls` (`public` label with `resolvePrincipal` in the handler, the scope event's tenant read off the row and every collection reached through `tenantRepo`, five `$in` reads independent of the batch size, the plan gate resolved once and only when a credentialed caller asks for an `original`, the handler's own `413` above 200 items, and `403 forbidden` only from `signedUrlBatchStatus`) | Accepted |
| [0768](ADR-0768-op128-account-status-gate-pins.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F1, High): the account-status gate on the media signer pinned — the `user` label's `evaluateAuth` table applied after `resolvePrincipal`, so a suspended / banned / deletion-pending caller is refused as the §5.6 code `forbidden` (`403`) with no `urls` and no signed object in the body, the per-item `forbidden` view being unobservable because the account-level refusal denies every item and `signedUrlBatchStatus` collapses that all-denied batch; the suspended matched attendee refused too, an `active` control signing both kinds, and a mixed batch still `200` | Accepted |
| [0770](ADR-0770-op128-account-status-gate-green.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F1, High) GREEN: the account-status gate implemented — `evaluateAuth("user", { principal, now: systemClock.now() })` after `resolvePrincipal`, a denial thrown as `appError("forbidden")` before any scope/image/asset read, so `suspended`/`deletion_pending`/`deleted`/unexpired-`banned` all answer `403 { error: { code: "forbidden" } }` while a `userProfiles.status = "active"` account keeps the per-item answer and the anonymous (I6) and OP-129 `attendee` paths are not gated; no read added, so I5's `reads(200) === reads(2)` holds | Accepted |
| [0771](ADR-0771-op128-signed-url-fallback-pins.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F2/F4/F5/F6 + F-G3, Low) TEST: the signed-URL fallbacks pinned and the U1 rationale corrected — the `.webp` fallback for an asset that records no `derivatives.thumbnail.format` (documented rather than denied, because the four §5.6 denial codes have no "derivative not ready yet" member, and pinned against the §4.4 projection's already-pinned `thumbnailUrl: null` so neither surface can drift alone), the image whose `media_assets` row is gone (`not_found`, alone `403`), a malformed / unknown scope id (per item `not_found`, credentialed `403`, anonymous `403`), A2 rewritten to the real precedence (`not_found` folds every out-of-scope reason but does separate "in scope" from "not in scope"), the U1 shape pin loosened through an `in` helper so `SignedUrlDecision` may become a discriminated union, the three stale `ADR-0764` spec references repointed to `ADR-0766`, and four breadth rows for the account gate (expired ban `200`, permanent ban `423 account_banned` with `details`, `deleted` `403`, suspended + 201 items `413` — the item cap runs before the gate) | Proposed |
| [0772](ADR-0772-op128-account-status-refusal-423-pins.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F-G1) TEST: the account-state refusal re-pinned to the **Appendix A.1** envelope — a whole-request refusal denies the request, not one `denied[]` item, so a `userProfiles.status = "suspended"` caller and a suspended **matched attendee** answer `423 account_suspended` with `details { reason: "suspended", supportUrl: "/support" }`, a Better Auth `user.banned` caller answers `423 account_banned` with `{ banReason, banExpires }` (an ISO string, or `null` for a permanent ban), and `403 forbidden` survives only for the deletion states (`deletion_pending`, `deleted`); the expired ban stays `200`, the item cap still answers `413` **before** the gate, the `active` control, the mixed batch and every no-`urls`/no-content-hash assertion are unchanged. Supersedes **ADR-0768 D1** and its "the wire carries no `supportUrl`/`banReason`/`banExpires`" consequence; ADR-0768 D2–D4 stand and the frozen record is not edited | Proposed |
| [0773](ADR-0773-op128-account-status-refusal-423-green.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F-G2 Green + the F-G1 pins' GREEN): the refusal action extracted as `enforceAuth(label, facts, request, route)` in `@/server/auth/guards` — `evaluateAuth`, one `warn` `auth.denied` `{label, reason: decision.code}` scoped to the route, then `appError(decision.code, …{details})`, which is the body `requireAuth` already ran and now calls — and `POST /api/v1/media/signed-urls` uses it in place of the inline `throw appError("forbidden")`, so a suspended / banned / deletion-pending refusal on the media signer emits the event security monitoring had lost and answers ADR-0772's Appendix A.1 envelope (`423 account_suspended` with `{reason, supportUrl}`, `423 account_banned` with `{banReason, banExpires}`, `403 forbidden` only for the deletion states) while the route stays `public` (I6's anonymous per-item `authentication_required`) and the `413` cap still precedes the gate; no new read, no contract change, and §5.6's all-denied `403` for an allowed principal is untouched — though the `auth.denied` line itself is still unpinned by a spec (recorded as a coverage gap) | Accepted |
| [0774](ADR-0774-op128-gate-413-no-signed-object-pin.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F1, Low) TEST: the sixth and last whole-request refusal row — `I7b`'s `413 payload_too_large` for a suspended caller's 201-item batch — now states the no-signed-object pair the other five refusal rows state (`expect(payload.urls).toBeUndefined()` and `expect(JSON.stringify(payload)).not.toContain(HASH_A)`), reading the body once through `body(response)` because `errorOf` consumes it; the cap still runs before the account gate (ADR-0772 D3), so the pin is near-vacuous and green on arrival, the case count is unchanged at 31 (one case body edited, no case added), and no production file, contract text or reviewed row is touched | Accepted |
| [0775](ADR-0775-op128-auth-denied-log-pin.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F1, Low) TEST: the media signer's account refusal pinned as **one** `warn` `auth.denied` `{label, reason}` entry with its `route` binding, and **none** for an allowed request — a capturing `memoryTransport` installed through `setLogger` (the `auth-guards.test.ts:583` shape, restored in a `finally`) captures the port while the suspended row still answers `423 account_suspended`, the `active` control answers `200` with an empty `denied[]` and adds no line, and neither caller's raw cookie appears in `JSON.stringify(sink.entries)`; the case count goes `31 → 32` with no delivered row edited, and the mutation probe (the pre-F-G2 inline `evaluateAuth` + same-envelope `throw`, no log) reddens **only** this case (`expected [] to have a length of 1 but got +0`) while the other 31 stay green — closes ADR-0773 O3, no production byte, no contract text | Accepted |
| [0776](ADR-0776-op128-gate-i4-no-signed-object-pin.md) | OP-128 follow-up (F3, Low) TEST: the seventh and last whole-request refusal row — `I4`'s `413 payload_too_large` for an **authorized organizer's** 201-item batch — now states the no-signed-object pair the other six state (`expect(payload.urls).toBeUndefined()` and `expect(JSON.stringify(payload)).not.toContain(HASH_A)`), reading the body once through `body(response)` with the `I7b` row's shape copied verbatim; the cap still runs before the account gate (ADR-0772 D3), so the pin is near-vacuous and green on arrival, the case count stays at 32 (one case body edited, no case added), the reachable mutation is a `details.entries[].contentHash` leak that reddens the hash line while a top-level `urls` body cannot reach the wire (the serializer rejects `error` + `urls` as `500`), closes **ADR-0774 O2**, and no production file, contract text or reviewed row is touched | Accepted |
| [0780](ADR-0780-op129-anonymous-attendee-sessions-red.md) | OP-129 RED: the anonymous attendee session contract — `opat_` token minting/hashing, the `op_att` cookie builder (env-scoped `Secure`), `POST /p/attendee-sessions` (hashed-at-rest row, TTL from `retention.attendeeSessionDays`, raw token issued once) and `GET /p/attendee-sessions/current` (cookie **or** `X-Attendee-Session`, `401 attendee_session_expired` vs `401 authentication_required`), pinned name-agnostically by a database-wide scanner | Accepted |
| [0781](ADR-0781-op129-anonymous-attendee-sessions-green.md) | OP-129 GREEN: the contract implemented — `attendeeSessions` in `COLLECTIONS` with the schema §13.5 index set, the token/cookie modules on the shipped `randomToken`/`sha256Hex` primitives, the resolver (cookie **or** header, expiry throws `attendee_session_expired` with `recreateUrl`) wired into the `attendee` label, both routes, the salted OP-89 device fingerprint, `eventSlug` bound through `events.accessLinks[].slug` (`404 not_found` when unknown) and `attendee_session_expired` appended to the auth code set | Accepted |
| [0782](ADR-0782-op129-attendee-session-edge-pins.md) | OP-129 round-1 review follow-up (test-only): **F2** the `eventSlug` binding pinned end to end — `201` with `eventIds` exactly the seeded event, `current` projecting the slug back, `404 not_found` for an unknown slug with **no** session row written (a collection-agnostic `tokenHash` scan before/after) and "only the named event" for a second seed; **F4** a stale `op_att` cookie must fall through to a valid `X-Attendee-Session` header (`200`, RED by design) with the lapsed-cookie answer (`401 attendee_session_expired` + `recreateUrl`, never a header retry) green beside it; **F5** a signed-in user is admitted by the `attendee` label and answered `401 authentication_required` (proved through the absent `auth.denied` record); **F3** `requireAuth("attendee", { auth, database })` with **no** injected resolver must authenticate a real `op_att` cookie (principal, admission, expiry semantics, unknown-token refusal) — RED by design, GREEN `t_a909e641` | Accepted |
| [0783](ADR-0783-op129-attendee-resolver-default.md) | OP-129 round-1 follow-up GREEN: **F3** the guard's default `attendeeResolver` is `createAttendeeSessionResolver({ database: options.database })`, built per request inside `resolveAttendee` from the handle the guard already has (never at module scope, so `next build`'s env-less page-data pass cannot fail), which retires the OP-86 `noAttendeeSessions` stub and lets `current` drop its duplicate wiring; **F4** `findRequestAttendeeSession` falls through from an unknown `op_att` cookie to the `X-Attendee-Session` header while `findAttendeeSession`'s expiry throw (`attendee_session_expired` + `recreateUrl`) and the unknown-header `401 authentication_required` stay exactly as shipped | Accepted |
| [0784](ADR-0784-op130-public-event-resolution-red.md) | OP-130 RED: the public landing contract — `formatStartAtLocal` (pure, numeric offset, DST-correct across the 2026 New York transitions) and `resolvePublicEventOutcome` (the §6.1 precedence table as data: unknown 404 → revoked 410 → draft 404 → deleting 410 → rendering window with inclusive retention), pinned through `GET /p/events/:slug` itself (six-key envelope, `description`, a **verified** thumbnail `logoUrl`, `hasProfile` from this event's profile row, a lapsed cookie still rendering, the §0.11 `public.gallery` class named by its `RateLimit-*` wire signature, and rotation old-410/new-200) | Accepted |
| [0785](ADR-0785-op130-public-event-resolution-green.md) | OP-130 GREEN: the landing call implemented — `@/server/attendee/public-event` exporting `formatStartAtLocal` (`Intl` `longOffset` at the instant, `h23`, `GMT` → `+00:00`, no milliseconds, no `Z`) and `resolvePublicEventOutcome` (the §6.1 table with refused-before-status revocation and an inclusive retention boundary), and `GET /api/v1/p/events/:slug` as the I/O projection: the active slug via `$elemMatch` on the schema §21 partial index with a `_id`-projected probe only on a miss (revoked `410` vs unknown `404`), refusals thrown as the bare `error` envelope (the revoked sentence in `details.message`, nothing else anywhere), the six-key body in a strict response schema, a signed `thumbnail` logo URL (extension from the derivative's `format`, `webp` default), the selfie budget from the tenant's `selfies.per_attendee` entitlement with a new `settings.selfie` fallback, the biometric policy from a new `settings.legal` section, `hasProfile` from this event's profile row, and a lapsed `op_att` cookie still rendering `200`; `event_gone` appended to `EVENT_ERROR_CODES`/`EVENT_ERROR_TRANSPORT` (`410`, not `ERROR_CATALOG`) and `description` added to contract §6.1's appendix | Accepted |
| [0786](ADR-0786-op129-attendee-session-rate-limit-green.md) | OP-129 follow-up (F1) GREEN: `attendee.session` implemented exactly as ADR-0787 declared it — the union member, the `{scope:"ip", limit:60, windowSeconds:3600}` table row and the `FAIL_CLOSED_CLASSES` entry in `classes.ts`, plus the `rateLimitStage({classKey, limiter: createRateLimiter(), salt})` option on the create route's `defineRoute` (the memoised accessor, because the fail-closed pin rejects on `createRateLimiter().limit`); the IP comes from the stage's own `resolveClientIp` + `getTrustedClientIpHeader()` derivation (ADR-0024 model unchanged, no route-local resolver added); token, TTL read, `eventSlug` binding and `201` body byte-identical; a `201` now carries `RateLimit-*` (accepted, ADR-0787 §4) | Accepted |
| [0787](ADR-0787-op129-attendee-session-rate-limit-pin.md) | OP-129 follow-up (F1): contract §0.11's `attendee.session` class for `POST /p/attendee-sessions` — `{scope:"ip", limit:60, windowSeconds:3600}` (the endpoint is public and runs **before** a session exists, so there is no principal, no contact and no attendee identity to key on), declared **fail-closed** (an unauthenticated call writes a 30-day credential-shaped row, so an unavailable limiter must not degrade into an unlimited mint), pinned in `classes.test.ts` (exact table row, exact `FAIL_CLOSED`/`FAIL_OPEN` partition) and in `attendee-sessions.test.ts` (60 `201`s then `429 rate_limited` with `RateLimit-*` + `Retry-After` and no minted row, a second IP unaffected at `RateLimit-Remaining: 59`, and `503 service_unavailable` when the limiter rejects on the memoised accessor); the GREEN declares the stage on the route's `defineRoute` options; takes `0787` because `0782` is held by the edge-pins record (PR #449) and `0783` by the resolver-default record — the number was minted as `0782` and renumbered by card `t_f4da0ad2` (review F1) | Accepted |
| [0788](ADR-0788-op129-attendee-credential-precedence-pin.md) | OP-129 round-1 review follow-up (test-only): **F3** the credential **order** pinned where it is load-bearing — a valid `op_att` cookie and a valid `X-Attendee-Session` header naming two _different_ sessions on one request answer `200` with the **cookie's** `id`, `kind: "anonymous"`, the cookie's `expiresAt` and neither raw token in the body; the four neighbouring cases (`I2`/`I3` single-credential, the stale-cookie fall-through, the lapsed-cookie `401`) are all blind to the order, so a header-first implementation passed them all — a throwaway-worktree mutation that prefers the header when both rows are live reddens **exactly** this case (`expected '…5b52' to be '…5b51'`, 1 failed \| 18 passed) and the shipped resolver is byte-identical; characterisation, so it passes on first run and needs no GREEN | Accepted |
| [0789](ADR-0789-op131-biometric-consent-red.md) | OP-131 RED: biometric consent grant and withdrawal — `buildConsentEvidence` (separate salted SHA-256 `ipHash`/`uaHash`, `method: "checkbox"`, a `parseLocale` locale, never a raw IP or user agent) and `formatDeletedCounts` (`selfies=n;embeddings=n;matches=n`, zeros spelled `0`, round-trippable), pinned through `POST`/`DELETE /p/events/:slug/consent` (the six-key `201`, a deterministic `sha256:` `policyDocumentHash` across two attendees, the `consentId`-linked profile, `422 policy_version_stale` and `422 age_confirmation_required` with **no** write, and a `204` + `X-Deleted-Counts` that leaves zero selfie/match rows and a released media `refCount`) plus `requireConsent` (`412 consent_required` with the Appendix A.7 `purpose`/`requiredPolicyVersion`/`policyUrl` details for a missing, a withdrawn and a stale-version grant) | Accepted |
| [0790](ADR-0790-op131-biometric-consent-green.md) | OP-131 GREEN: the consent contract implemented and the six ADR-0789 open questions answered — `buildConsentEvidence` on two independent `hashFingerprint` calls (the session-fingerprint scheme and deployment salt, never a raw IP or user agent) and `formatDeletedCounts` from a template literal; `POST /p/events/:slug/consent` on the `attendee` label + `write.normal`, a literal `purpose`/`granted: true` body with `ageConfirmed` optional, the contract-order refusals (`422 policy_version_stale` then `422 age_confirmation_required`, neither writing), a `sha256:` `policyDocumentHash` over the version+URL pair (the settings document carries no body to hash — the upgrade trigger is recorded), a read-then-write profile link that keeps a `ready` profile's `activeSelfieId`/`matchCount` and restarts only a `withdrawn` one, and a **second grant inserting a second row** (the guard reads the latest); `DELETE` scoped by `profileId`, counting only the selfies that carried an `embedding`, `refCount - 1` under a `> 0` guard with the blob left to the sweep, every live consent stamped `withdrawnAt` and the profile `withdrawn`, answering `204` + the driver's own `deletedCount`s, and **idempotent** (`204` with zeros when nothing is left, so a client can always retry); `requireConsent` reads the current policy then the latest non-withdrawn row at that version, so a withdrawal and a retired version are refused structurally; `CONSENT_ERROR_CODES` + `CONSENT_ERROR_TRANSPORT` appended (`consent_required` 412, `policy_version_stale` 422, `age_confirmation_required` 422 — not `ERROR_CATALOG`), `consents`/`selfies` registered in `COLLECTIONS`, and contract §6.2/A.7 given the `ageConfirmed` field and its row | Accepted |
| [0791](ADR-0791-op132-liveness-selfie-red.md) | OP-132 RED: liveness challenge, selfie submit/confirm, the attempt budget and reuse — two pure modules (`createLivenessChallenge`/`decideLivenessChallenge` with an injectable RNG, a 3-gesture/15 s spec, a 60 s life and a 32-hex evidence-binding `nonce`; `selfieAttemptBudget` clamping the remainder at 0 and echoing `used` verbatim) and six routes pinned at the observable level: `POST …/liveness/challenges` (six keys, `412 consent_required` with the A.7 details), `POST …/selfies/uploads` (the five-key single-PUT envelope, `413 file_too_large`), `POST …/selfies` (seven-key `202` with `pollUrl`/`pollIntervalMs: 2000`/`attempts`, `processing.{status:"queued", priority:"high", queueRef:{queueKey:"q:selfie", provider:"memory"}}`, one §10.4 envelope at submit time, `409 already_used`/`mismatch`, `403 plan_limit_exceeded` on the fourth attempt with a second attendee keeping their own budget, `422` for missing evidence, `412 consent_required` before the object is looked at), `POST …/selfies/{selfieId}/confirm` (`200` + `status: "ready"` once the worker's embedding is on the row, `isActive`/`activeSelfieId`/`matchCount` only then, exactly one `attendee.matches.new`), `GET /p/selfies/reusable` (signed previews, no embedding, `401` for an anonymous session) and `POST …/selfies:reuse` (`202`, cross-event consent `412`, a foreign id `404`, a withdrawn id `404`); the colon verb is mapped to a nested segment because Next cannot express `[selfieId]:confirm`; 27 unit + 25 integration + 1 e2e cases, three mutations each reddening exactly one named case, and the e2e reaches its own `201` assertion through the real HTTP surface | Accepted (RED / executable contract) |
| [0792](ADR-0792-op132-liveness-selfie-green.md) | OP-132 GREEN: the liveness/selfie contract implemented and ADR-0791's six open questions answered — `createLivenessChallenge`/`decideLivenessChallenge` (injectable RNG, 3 gestures from a 4-member vocabulary, 15 s budget, 60 s life, a 32-hex `crypto` nonce, precedence `already_used` → `expired` → `mismatch`, inclusive deadline, case-insensitive nonce) and `selfieAttemptBudget` (remainder clamped at 0); the challenge stored as a **capped five-element list** on `attendeeEventProfiles` (no collection) spent by one conditional `usedAt: null` update inside the submit transaction, so a replay loses the write; `POST …/selfies` in the contract order (consent → `headObject` → challenge → budget) then one transaction (profile upsert `processing`, a `queued`/`high` `selfies` row keyed by `selfies/<sha256>`, the challenge's `usedAt`) and the `q:selfie` §10.4 pointer after the commit; `POST …/selfies/{selfieId}/confirm` the only publisher (`isActive`, `activeSelfieId`, `matchCount`, `status: "ready"` and the single `attendee.matches.new`), gated on the worker's `embedding` so a pre-worker confirm only activates the row; `…/selfies:reuse` resolving consent for the addressed event, the source selfie by own subject and `isActive` (a foreign or withdrawn id `404`), and re-processing the bytes instead of copying a vector; `GET /p/selfies/reusable` as `crossTenantReads.findReusableSelfiesBySubjectUser`, which now matches `subject.userId` as an `ObjectId` **or** a string (schema §10.3 stores the id; the OP-77 fixtures seeded strings) and projects id/`createdAt`/`contentHash`/quality only; the attendee-scoped rate classes declared as the post-auth `rateLimitIdentity` stage keyed on the server-resolved session (an anonymous caller publishes no principal, so the `op_att` session is re-resolved); `SELFIE_ERROR_CODES` + `SELFIE_ERROR_TRANSPORT` for `liveness_challenge_invalid` (409, `{reason}`) and the consent route's event/subject helpers moved to `request-event.ts`/`subject.ts`; out of scope by the RED's own evidence rule: `liveness_failed`, the frame verifier, the TTL purge, `gallery_closed`, replay and `GET /me/events` | Accepted |
| [0793](ADR-0793-op133-attendee-gallery-red.md) | OP-133 RED: the attendee read surface pinned as an executable contract — a pure poll table (`2000`/`15000`/`60000`, `nextPollAfter` non-mutating), `displayGeometry` transposing EXIF `5`–`8` and rounding the ratio to four decimals, `resolveStatus` (`ready`+0 → `no_match`/`no_matches_yet`, four stable `failed` codes, everything else collapsed to `processing_failed`), and the optional `download` name bound into the §5.7 signature (byte-identical when absent); the gallery ordered by `event_images.sequence` desc (I1 makes `createdAt` run opposite, so a timestamp sort fails), `faceMatches.hiddenAt` excluded **and counted** vs `event_images.visibility: hidden` excluded **and not counted** (I1/I1b, the OP-127 follow-up), the twelve-key item with verified thumbnails and no `similarity`, the anonymous `attendee.anonymousPreviewLimit` truncation (default `10`, `truncatedForAnonymous` + `loginUrl`, comparator cursor `422`), 45-match pagination and a 6-item walk that survives two mid-flight inserts; `/me` as §6.4's fourteen keys plus `latestMatchCursor`, `GET` never clearing the badges, `seenAt` leaving later matches `isNew`, `?newerThan=` fetching only the new items; the media refusals (unmatched `403`, unknown `404`, plan gate `feature_not_available`, owner-only download job `404`) and the test-only `POST /api/v1/test-support/attendee-matches` fixture with its `404` outside `test`/`e2e`; E1 drives the whole journey over HTTP | Accepted |
| [0794](ADR-0794-op133-attendee-gallery-green.md) | OP-133 GREEN: the attendee read surface implemented and ADR-0793's open questions answered — the venue-neutral poll table (`2000`/`15000`/`60000`) plus `eventIndexingComplete`, `displayGeometry` returning the displayed pair rather than the stored one, `resolveStatus` populating `statusDetail` for two states only, the download filename bound into the §5.7 preimage (the signature is byte-identical when no name is passed) and a `ceil`-rounded `exp` so a stated `expiresAt` is a lower bound on the URL's life; `event_images.sequence` as the gallery's keyset ordering key with two cursor kinds (`cursor` pages, `newerThan` filters — a `newerThan` cursor is not a `cursor`), the two hide semantics and their two counts (an attendee hide is counted, an image-visibility hide is not), the anonymous allowance as a **total** rather than a page size, the platform-scope `downloadJobs` row whose id is never the authorization, the guarded test-only matcher fixture, the event-before-credential order that keeps `404` and `401` apart, the optional-body reader the two `{…?}` routes need, and the two new `face_matches` indexes plus the `downloadJobs` TTL. One pin was unsatisfiable as written — I7 compared `keysOf(body)` (sorted) against an unsorted expected array — and was corrected by the Test Author (card `t_c6d20dea`) by sorting the expected side, keeping the assertion an exact five-key set | Accepted |
| [0796](ADR-0796-op132-selfie-asset-owner-expiry-purge.md) | OP-132 GREEN (F1, High): the selfie's owner row, its retention deadline and the purge path — `insertQueuedSelfie` upserts **one** `mediaAssets` row per `(tenant, contentHash)` (`kind: "selfie"`, `status: "stored"`, `$setOnInsert` for the identity fields and `$inc: {refCount: 1}` so the unique index arbitrates a repeat submit or a reuse as a **second reference**) and stores its id on the `selfies` row's `assetId`; the queued row carries `expireAt = createdAt + 90 d` capped by the event's own `retentionExpiresAt` (schema §22 "whichever first", read off the event row `resolveEventBySlug` already loads — no `retention.*` setting names a selfie window, so 90 is `SELFIE_RETENTION_DAYS`); the asset row deliberately carries **no `storage`** sub-document because §16.1's `contentHash` _is_ the object key; `DELETE …/consent` now counts one reference **per deleted selfie row**, decrements under the same `refCount > 0` guard and, at `<= 0`, deletes `originals/selfies/<sha256>` **synchronously** (the pin reads the object back through `getObjectStorage()` after the `204`), deriving the key from the content hash — reading `asset.storage.objectKey` answers `500` on the OP-131 fixture, which has no `storage` slot (measured); the asset row itself survives at `refCount: 0`, as OP-131 pins | Accepted |
| [0797](ADR-0797-op132-failed-purge-retry-pin.md) | OP-132 TEST (F-A2, High): pin the failed-purge retry and the purge/reference race at the invariant — a withdrawal whose `deleteObject` rejected once and is then retried must leave **no orphaned selfie object** (the bytes gone, **or** the `mediaAssets` row a durable purge candidate at `refCount: 0` with `status: "purged"`/`purgedAt` a sweep can act on, derived without the `selfies` rows the failed attempt already deleted), while the retry keeps the withdrawal's own contract (`204` + `X-Deleted-Counts`, read as a format because a rollback repair retries with the rows in place and a ledger repair finds them gone); and a second attendee's submit of the same bytes driven from **inside** the `deleteObject` stub must never leave a `202` reference pointing at deleted bytes — the "bytes stay readable" branch alone is unsatisfiable once the purge is committed to, so the pin is `202 ∧ bytes gone → fail`, with a non-vacuity reader that the delete was really reached; `shortcut:` (ADR-0796 §3) is now pinned rather than recorded. Two throwaway repairs prove satisfiability and independence: a purge-candidate fix turns P1 green and leaves P2 red, a claim-before-delete + refuse-a-purged-row fix turns both green with the 19 frozen cases still green, and that probe measured the losing submit answering `500` (the `(tenantId, contentHash)` unique index) — handed to the GREEN as an open question, not pinned. Deliberately not pinned: the `status` of the surviving `refCount: 0` row after a successful purge, and two concurrent submits of one hash (no synchronous seam, flaky by construction). | Accepted (RED / executable contract) |
| [0798](ADR-0798-op132-purge-before-delete-claim.md) | OP-132 GREEN (F-A2, High): the failed selfie-blob purge made retryable — `DELETE …/consent` purges **before** it deletes the rows: per referenced asset one atomic claim (`findOneAndUpdate({_id, refCount: {$lte: count}}, {$set: {refCount: 0, status: "purged", purgedAt: null}})`, so no row is ever observed at `refCount: 0` while `stored`), then `deleteObject("originals", selfies/<contentHash>)`, then `$set purgedAt`, and only then the `selfies`/`faceMatches` deletes, the `withdrawnAt` stamp and the profile — so a failed delete leaves the rows **and** a durable candidate behind and the retry re-enters the claim branch, re-attempts the object and reports the rows it finally deletes (the first attempt still answers `500`); `insertQueuedSelfie` refuses a claim in flight (`status: "purged"` and `purgedAt: null`) with `409 conflict {details: {reason: "selfie_purged"}}` before the upsert, so a submit racing the delete is deflected instead of being handed a reference to dying bytes (the reviewer's `E11000` probe answered `500` and spent the challenge; this one answers `409`), while a completed purge (`purgedAt` set) leaves a re-upload a normal submit; happy path unchanged (`204`, the same `X-Deleted-Counts`, the row kept at `refCount: 0`, the seven-key envelope, no existing status code moved); no queue entry and no sweep — the withdrawal retry is the consumer `t_6b53935c` will pin | Accepted |
| [0799](ADR-0799-op133-preview-url-selfie-summary-pins.md) | OP-133 follow-up (TEST): the two success paths PR #547 shipped unpinned. `POST …/images/{imageId}/preview-url` now pins its `201` answer — the three §6.6 keys, `ttlSeconds: 7200`, a `/p/{hash}.jpg` pathname (so a signature over the original's `/o/` segment fails), a future whole-second `exp`, a `sig` that **verifies** with the app's own signer and an `expiresAt` bracketing `now + ttlSeconds` — and, because ADR-0794 §13 gives a preview **no** `originals.download` gate, the plan refusal is pinned at its real home: one plan that withholds originals still grants the preview and still refuses the original `403 feature_not_available`. The three refusals `requireSignableImage` fixes in order are pinned too — unknown id `404 not_found`, visible-but-unmatched `403 forbidden`, an organizer-hidden image `403 forbidden` **even for a matched caller**, and the anonymous-session `403 forbidden {reason: "identified_only"}` that separates it from the no-credential `401`. `/me`'s `selfie` field is typed (it was `unknown`) and pinned from an **activated** selfie driven through the shipped submit → worker → confirm path: the exact seven-key summary, the budget at one attempt of three, a verified `/t/{hash}.webp` thumbnail that is never the original key, `selfie: null` with the `no_selfie` wire status before any submission, and two polls that return the same row without clearing a badge or spending an attempt. No production file is touched; five one-line mutations each redden exactly their case | Accepted |
| [0800](ADR-0800-op132-selfies-ttl-and-kind-dedupe-pins.md) | OP-132 TEST (F-A3/F-A4, Medium): the two schema-conformance gaps the F1 GREEN made reachable, pinned where they are observable. F-A3 — the retention deadline `insertQueuedSelfie` stamps is inert because the registry declares **no** `selfies` index set, so the pin asserts exactly one `selfies` TTL index, `keys: [["expireAt", 1]]` with `expireAfterSeconds: 0` (§16.4, §22's TTL column — `selfies` is _not_ one of the collections §22 routes to an application purge job), asserted on `INDEX_SPECS` with no database. F-A4 — the tenant-wide dedupe key is `[tenantId, contentHash]`, so an organizer upload of bytes identical to a submitted selfie resolves the selfie's deliberately storage-less asset and `complete-upload` reads `existingAsset.storage` unguarded: measured on the branch cut, that completion answers `500 internal_error` (logged `TypeError: … reading 'bytes'` at `toEventImage` ← `completeStoredUpload:331`) _after_ its transaction committed, and the selfie asset's `refCount` moves `1 → 2`. The index pin asserts the invariant — no unique `media_assets` index may be kind-blind (either `kind` as a key, the encoding this lane expects, or a unique index made partial on `kind`) — and integration case I4 asserts it behaviourally: two rows differing only by `kind` must both insert (today `E11000` on `media_assets_tenant_content_hash_unique`, `keyPattern {tenantId: 1, contentHash: 1}`). The collision is pinned as **success**, not a refusal: `201` with the §5.3 DTO, the `eventImages` row's own `kind: "event_image"` asset carrying the `storage` descriptor (`objectKey: images/<hash>.jpg`, `bytes`), and the selfie's row untouched (`refCount: 1`, no descriptor) — a refusal was rejected because §16.1's dedupe is an organiser-side optimisation, not a licence to serve an attendee's biometric asset to a gallery. A three-file near-fix (the `selfies` TTL spec, `kind` in the unique key, both asset reads scoped by kind) turns 2 + 4 pins green with the four frozen attendee and three frozen upload specs still green, each file restored byte-identically; the migration is a bounded `ensureIndexes` drop-and-rebuild that no duplicate can block. Draws `0800` from the head of the pool (the attendee block `0780`–`0798` is exhausted; ADR-0799's precedent), both cells advance to `0801`. | Accepted (RED / executable contract) |
| [0801](ADR-0801-op132-selfies-ttl-and-kind-dedupe-green.md) | OP-132 GREEN (F-A3/F-A4, Medium): the six pins made true with no test file touched. The registry gains exactly the `selfies` TTL spec schema §16.4/§22 assign to the TTL monitor (`keys: [["expireAt", 1]]`, `expireAfterSeconds: 0`, named `selfies_expire_at_ttl`; no `TENANT_SCOPED_COLLECTIONS` entry, since the set's U1 lint governs compound indexes and this one is single-key). The tenant-wide dedupe key becomes `[tenantId, kind, contentHash]` — encoding (a) — under its **existing name**: `ensureIndexes` reconciles the drifted definition by drop-and-rebuild but never drops an index the registry no longer declares, so a rename would leave the old two-key unique index live wherever the bootstrap does not rerun. Every reader and writer of the tenant-wide hash now names its own kind — `complete-upload`'s `settledCompletion` and `existingAsset` read (`kind: "event_image"`) and `selfie-submit`'s purge-claim read and owner-row upsert (`kind: "selfie"`) — so the organizer completion inserts its own descriptor-bearing row (`201`, no `500`, the selfie's row untouched at `refCount: 1`) and the reverse order (ADR-0800 §5's unpinned hazard, and §Open question 1) is handled by construction. The index change is a migration with a required per-environment step — `pnpm db:ensure-indexes` (`scripts/db/ensure-indexes.ts`) reports `db.indexes.reconciled` and rebuilds the index under its name — with no backfill: the old index already forbade the duplicate a rebuild could hit, `kind` is a field every row carries, and the new key only relaxes uniqueness. Draws `0801` because the sweep found `0800` in flight **twice** (this lane's pins PR #552 and the OP-133 soft-delete pins PR #551), a ledger collision flagged for the reviewer; both cells advance to `0802`. §16.1's written key list is now stale and its owner should carry `kind` into it. | Accepted |

| [0803](ADR-0803-op132-reuse-attempt-cap-and-rate-pins.md) | OP-132 TEST (F2, Medium): the reuse write's two missing limits, pinned at the observable level in a new integration file — (1) an attendee whose target-event profile already holds `selfies.per_attendee` attempts and asks to reuse a confirmed selfie from another event gets `403 plan_limit_exceeded` with `details.entitlementKey` and mints **nothing** (no fourth `selfies` row, no new `q:selfie` pointer); (2) reuse and submit draw on one budget (a mixed submit→reuse→submit sequence reports `attemptsUsed` 1/2/3 and the fourth call on either path is the same refusal); (3) five concurrent submits with distinct `Idempotency-Key` values on `allowed = 3` and two seeded attempts mint exactly one attempt — one `202`, four `403`s, three stored rows, one queue entry — which the read-outside-the-transaction check cannot hold (measured `[202, 202, 202, 500, 500]`, the two `500`s being `TransactionRetryLimitError` from the racers' own profile upserts); (4) the reuse route mounts a §0.11 limiter, pinned **class-agnostically** because §0.11 has no reuse row: the `RateLimit-*` trio with `Remaining` counting down per call, and `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After` past the table's loosest POST class (`write.normal`, 60/min). A two-file near-fix (budget + `rateLimitIdentity` on reuse; a per-profile serializer around a budget re-read on submit) turns 5/5 green with both production files restored byte-identically, so every pin is satisfiable and none passes vacuously; the suite-wide `selfie.submit` token arithmetic (6/hour per attendee) is why case 3's spent attempts are seeded and the object is put through the storage port. Drawn `0803` because `0802` is in flight (the OP-133 soft-delete pins); both cells advance to `0804`. The class itself is **not** pinned: the GREEN records it and the DOCS card amends §0.11. | Accepted (RED / executable contract) |
| [0804](ADR-0804-op132-reuse-attempt-cap-and-rate-green.md) | OP-132 GREEN (F2, Medium): the five ADR-0803 pins made true with no test touched — `selfie-submit.ts` gains `chargeSelfieAttempt`, one critical section per profile (an in-process queue keyed by tenant + event + subject, `seed-plans.ts`'s `runExclusive` shape) that resolves the budget, refuses an exhausted one with `403 plan_limit_exceeded`/`selfies.per_attendee`, and only then writes the profile upsert, the queued `selfies` row and the caller's `commit` (the submit route's challenge single-use) in one transaction, so reuse is charged like a submit and concurrent submits cannot both take the last attempt (measured previously: `[202, 202, 202, 500, 500]`, the read-inside-the-transaction repair included); `…/selfies:reuse` keeps its consent → ownership `404` → source precedence and mounts `rateLimitIdentity: attendeeRateLimitStage("selfie.submit")` — the class every selfie write in this lane declares, 6/hour per attendee session and 20/hour per IP, fail-closed, denied with `429 rate_limited` + `Retry-After`; envelope, status codes and the seven keys unchanged, and the serializer is per process (`shortcut:` recorded, an atomic claim needs a counter schema §15.3 does not have). Draws `0804` (`0803` is this branch's pins, `0802` in flight on the OP-133 soft-delete pins); both cells advance to `0805`. | Accepted |

The GREEN implements the ADR-0342 pin exactly (option (a)): `consumeEntitlement` now branches on the resolved scope and returns `allowedVerdict(resolved, resolved.used)` for an `event`/`attendee`-scoped key, so the verdict's `used` is the caller's `ctx.counts` (the value `decide` reasoned about) and no `usageCounters` write is issued — `advanceCounter` is reachable only for `tenant` scope, the only scope `resolveEntitlement` reads a counter for. `advanceCounter` no longer falls back to `retried ?? (await readCounterValue(...))`: it returns `number | null`, `null` when and only when neither conditional `findOneAndUpdate` matched, which (given the filter's `value: { $lte: limit - amount }` and the upsert-first attempt) proves the counter advanced past the room between the decision and the write. On `null`, `consumeEntitlement` re-resolves against the live counter and denies with the fresh state (`deniedVerdict(reread, limitDenialReason(reread))`) — emitting `usage.action.blocked` outside the transaction and throwing the Appendix A error, the same enforcement surface as a decision-time denial — rather than reporting success off the stale read. The hard-limit denial reason (`payment_downgrade` vs `plan_limit`) and the emit-then-throw sequence are extracted into `limitDenialReason(...)` and a private `deny(...)` shared by both denial paths (behaviour-preserving). `releaseEntitlement` still writes the counter for every scope: the pins cover `consumeEntitlement` only, so this GREEN does not touch `release` — recorded as an open handoff gap for the Test Author rather than fixed untested. The GREEN implements the ADR-0380 contract in three new modules — the types-only port `@/server/payments/payment-provider`, the wire schemas in `adapters/cashfree/schemas.ts` and the adapter factory `createCashfreeProvider(config)` in `adapters/cashfree/cashfree-provider.ts` — with the twelve-row status table and the four-code failure table as `ReadonlyMap`s (so no vendor value is reachable through a computed object key), the seven vendor event names declared once, `base64(HMAC-SHA256(secret, x-webhook-timestamp + rawBody))` over the raw bytes inside the ±300 s window returning a plain `boolean`, the §10.1.2 projection with minor-unit amounts and `cf_payment_id` / `cf_refund_id` as `providerEventId`, the env-derived base URL, the pinned `x-api-version` / `x-client-id` / `x-client-secret` headers, `x-idempotency-key` on `chargeOnce`, the shared `classifyTransportFailure` for non-2xx (5xx/408/429 retryable), a per-call `AbortController` timeout with **no** automatic retry, the card's `payments.call` info line (operation, status, durationMs — added because the card asks for it and the I-specs exercise it) and the `payments.upstream_contract_violation` error line with `provider` + `operation`. Two questions the RED left open are settled in the ADR: the `kind` of a `subscription_status` change is derived from the **normalized** status (reproducing all five pinned §10.1.2 rows and keeping the event and the status pair in lockstep), and a payment event that omits its amount projects onto a zero-amount transaction rather than failing the whole webhook (a dropped money event cannot be retried; a durable row can be reconciled). **One deliberate departure from the card's §1**: `createPlan` and `cancelMandate` are **not** on the port, because ADR-0380 lists both as out of scope and no test in this card drives them — an untested vendor call is the next cycle's liability, and their wire contracts belong with their own pins (OP-107 / OP-110). _The **OP-105** lane `Next free` cell advanced from `0392` to `0393` (card `t_5547cab6`, OP-105 follow-up TEST — pin `createMandate().expiresAt` as RFC 3339 UTC ms `Z`; branch `OP-105-task-expiresat-rfc3339-pins`, worktree `/root/openpic/openpic-webapp/.worktrees/t_e553b2c9`, off a fresh `origin/main` `2a5f40e`, the `1.35.0` release on top of the request-projections squash `841f342`). `0380`–`0389` are consumed on the trunk (the port RED/GREEN pair, the live-sandbox record, the memory-provider RED/GREEN pair, the F2 drift-log pair and the F1 import-boundary pair) and `0390`/`0391` by [ADR-0390](ADR-0390-op105-adapter-request-projections-pin.md) / [ADR-0391](ADR-0391-op105-adapter-request-projections-green.md) — all count as taken under [ADR-0300](ADR-0300-adr-number-range-allocation-policy.md) §7 — so this record takes `0392`, the number the orchestrator reserved for this card (decision `t_e5485868`), and advances the cell to `0393`. [ADR-0392](ADR-0392-op105-expiresat-rfc3339-pin.md) pins finding **F1 — Medium** of the round-1 (artifact lens) review of the merged OP-105 live-sandbox card `t_0998dd27` (PR [#295](https://github.com/yahodu/openpic-webapp/pull/295), squash `a27f360`), routed by the orchestrator decision card `t_d32c89cf`: `createMandate().expiresAt` had no pinned format, so the vendor's value could reach §3.3's `checkout.expiresAt` unnormalized once OP-107 landed. The routing card recalled a space-separated `"2023-05-30 00:00:00"` (no `T`, no zone) — that recollection is **wrong**: the current Create Subscription reference types `subscription_expiry_time` as `string<ISO8601>` and documents `"We store timestamps in IST"` with the example `"2025-06-01T10:20:12+05:30"`, i.e. ISO 8601 with an explicit IST `+05:30` offset, second precision, no ms and no `Z`. The decision is unchanged (the offset-bearing form is still neither UTC nor ms precision); the pin drives that shape. New spec `apps/web/src/test/integration/payments-cashfree-expiresat.test.ts` (MSW): I1 asserts, per row and **on the input itself**, that the vendor string is offset-bearing with neither ms nor `Z` (so the pin cannot be weakened to an ISO-ms fixture) and that `expiresAt` comes back as the RFC 3339 UTC ms `Z` value (`2025-06-01T10:20:12+05:30` → `2025-06-01T04:50:12.000Z`, the vendor docs' own example; `2100-01-01T23:00:08+05:30` → `2100-01-01T17:30:08.000Z`; the factory default `2026-11-01T10:30:46+05:30` → `2026-11-01T05:00:46.000Z`) plus the canonical regex; I2 pins that an unparseable vendor expiry rejects with `UpstreamContractError` rather than returning raw garbage (mirroring `parseWebhook`'s unparseable-`event_time` guard). The pre-existing verbatim-passthrough assertion in `payments-cashfree-adapter.test.ts` (`toBe(response.subscription_expiry_time)`) contradicted the decision and would have blocked the GREEN, so its expectation is repinned to `2026-11-01T05:00:46.000Z`; the factory fixture stays vendor-shaped, untouched. Evidence at the RED head: integration **5 failed | 5 passed (10)** — all five at assertion level against today's passthrough (`expected '2025-06-01T10:20:12+05:30' to be '2025-06-01T04:50:12.000Z'`; `expected '2026-11-01T10:30:46+05:30' to be '2026-11-01T05:00:46.000Z'`; `promise resolved "{ … }" instead of rejecting`) — `npx tsc -p apps/web/tsconfig.json --noEmit` exit `0`, ESLint 0 problems and `prettier --check` clean on both touched specs. **Test files only** — no production file touched, no test-mode conditional.
